What this evaluates

(agb 0a8c4ee7) Does agb's Claude Code skill pack make Claude better at each agb stage? Same model and starting tree (agb's adopted output of the earlier stages), with the agb-skills plugin running /agb-<stage> or without any plugin. Planning steps: passes when the deliverables parse, agb's required gates are the score, pairwise judge on substance. Code: held-out acceptance tests.

This step (test-design): Test design: write a test plan (.ab/test-plan.json) that covers every acceptance criterion.

A pass of test-plan-gates means: Arm confirmed from the trace (else void). Passes when .ab/test-plan.json lists cases; score: share clean of no hard ac-coverage finding, every AC covered.

directional

Recommend opus-5-5-skill · highest assurance

$3.0511 per success · required check 3/3 passed · check score 100%

4 eligible candidates tie at 1.000 (3/3) reliability and 1.000 (3/3) required-check pass rate (within the minimum meaningful difference 0.05); opus-5-5-skill is picked by the judge's pairwise score (opus-5-5-skill 100%, next sonnet-5-5-skill 50%).

Why, in the judge's words: On demo-todo, against sonnet-5-5-skill: A provides stronger implementation guards through explicit rollback assertions in AT-024 and AT-049 and concurrent-update checks in AT-019 and AT-034, which B lacks. Although B consistently supplies covers anchors, A's concrete failure-state coverage and separation of server, UI, and cross-page checks outweigh its traceability gaps.

The judge preferred opus-5-5-skill most (9 W 0 L 0 T of 9), which matches the recommendation.

Directional only: only 3 input(s) (fewer than 6). Also opus-5-5-skill: required-check-pass-rate 3/3 has a 95% interval down to 0.439, below the gate 1; a gate of 1 can never be firm, declare one such as 0.95; opus-5-5-skill: reliability 3/3 has a 95% interval down to 0.439, below the gate 1; a gate of 1 can never be firm, declare one such as 0.95.

Cost and success one point per candidate · up passes the required check more often, left costs less per success

The cost is the generation cost per success the operating mode ranks on: under "lowest cost" the recommendation is the leftmost filled point above the gate. A point can sit above the gate and still be hollow when another gate (reliability, pass^k) removed it; hover a point for its numbers and the gate that removed it.

Selection each eligibility gate removes candidates, the operating mode picks among the rest · rule select-v4

  1. required-check pass rate ≥ 100% — removed: nobody removed. Remaining: opus-5-5-skill, opus-5-5-none, sonnet-5-5-skill, sonnet-5-5-none.
  2. reliability ≥ 100% — removed: nobody removed. Remaining: opus-5-5-skill, opus-5-5-none, sonnet-5-5-skill, sonnet-5-5-none.

Passed every gate: opus-5-5-skill, opus-5-5-none, sonnet-5-5-skill, sonnet-5-5-none. The judge's win rate is for reference only and takes no part in selection.

Judge scores pairwise win rate · not used by the recommendation

No 1–5 scores: this step's methods does not declare absolute-1-5. That was declared, not missed.

Pairwise win rate (the table above) is a separate question: shown two outputs side by side, which did the judge prefer, 0–100, a tie counting 0.5. It only counts the matches each candidate took part in, so 0 means "lost every match", not "bad output".

Match evidence each dimension judged separately; an order disagreement is recorded as a tie by rule

opus-5-5-skill vs opus-5-5-none demo-todo opus-5-5-skill

A provides stronger implementation guards through AT-019/AT-034 concurrency invariants and AT-022 server-side edit validation, which B lacks. B has better explicit traceability, but its repeated mocked failure checks in AT-007/AT-008 and incorrect workload expectation in AT-054 make it the weaker suite overall.

coverageopus-5-5-skill A AT-019 and AT-034 test concurrent member deletion/assignment and competing state transitions; B tests those operations separately but has no corresponding concurrency checks. A also tests invalid PATCH titles and nonexistent assignees in AT-022 and description-save rollback in AT-026, whereas B's detail tests omit those cases.
falsifiableopus-5-5-skill A AT-023 requires PATCHing status to return 400, while B AT-016 accepts either rejection or ignoring the field; similarly, A AT-030 requires 409 for forbidden transitions while B AT-021 accepts 409/400. A AT-050 explicitly keeps workload at 1 when starting an assigned task, consistent with both plans' aggregation rules, whereas B AT-054 incorrectly expects a sidebar +1 at that step.
levelsopus-5-5-skill B repeats the same creation-failure assertions in mocked page test AT-007 and mocked e2e AT-008, and repeats default completed-task hiding in AT-029 and AT-031. A tests these locally in AT-015 and AT-037, then uses AT-050 for a connected creation-to-completion flow rather than adding separate e2e repetitions of each assertion.
traceableopus-5-5-none Every B case supplies a nonempty covers array and an implementation-area test file, such as AT-018 mapping title-save behavior to AC-05 and TaskDetailPage.test.tsx. A leaves covers empty for title saving, description saving, and missing-task handling in AT-025 through AT-027, and supplies no comparable per-case file or task mapping.
opus-5-5-skill vs sonnet-5-5-skill demo-todo opus-5-5-skill

A provides stronger implementation guards through explicit rollback assertions in AT-024 and AT-049 and concurrent-update checks in AT-019 and AT-034, which B lacks. Although B consistently supplies covers anchors, A's concrete failure-state coverage and separation of server, UI, and cross-page checks outweigh its traceability gaps.

coverageopus-5-5-skill A tests failure and pending states absent from B: AT-009 checks failed member deletion preserves rows, AT-024 checks reassignment rollback, and AT-049 checks failed advancement preserves the card and workload. A also tests conflicting transitions and member-deletion/task-creation races in AT-034 and AT-019; B tests ordinary transitions and deletion but neither race.
falsifiableopus-5-5-skill A AT-013 specifies 400 responses and unchanged task counts for enumerated invalid inputs, whereas B AT-026 accepts any 4xx and describes the database outcome only as data not being corrupted. For filtering, A AT-044 gives exact count changes from 4 to 2 and 1 to 1, while B AT-016 says column counts synchronize without specifying their expected values.
levelsopus-5-5-skill A separates server transition enforcement in AT-029–031, rendered transition controls in in-process AT-032, and a cross-page real-backend flow in AT-050. B uses real-backend e2e AT-009 merely to count transition buttons and mocked e2e AT-001 for local title validation, making browser-level tests carry checks A handles with narrower UI integration tests.
traceablesonnet-5-5-skill Every B case has a nonempty covers array, whereas A leaves concrete behaviors unanchored, including title and description saving in AT-025–026, navigation in AT-040, and current-member highlighting in AT-046. B's mappings are not uniformly precise—AT-024 links the 200-task API check to AC-09—but A has substantially more tests with no criterion link at all.

traceableopus-5-5-none A AT-010 anchors the empty-client-list interaction directly to AC-004, while B additionally anchors name/email search and status filtering to that same criterion in AT-045 without establishing their connection to the empty-state behavior. Likewise, A AT-041 maps dashboard emptiness to AC-027, whereas B AT-053 attaches unspecified dashboard metric calculations to that anchor; both provide covers and file paths, but B has more loosely attached additions.
sonnet-5-5-skill vs sonnet-5-5-none invoice sonnet-5-5-skill

A supplies materially stronger guards through crash recovery (AT-073), email failure (AT-081), payout reconciliation (AT-082), and recurring date boundaries (AT-075), not merely additional happy-path tests. Its separate API and UI tests for token validation and frozen-client rejection also make the intended assertions more executable than B's mixed-level AT-006–008; B's explicit file mappings do not outweigh those differences.

coveragesonnet-5-5-skill A tests failure and recovery cases absent from B: AT-073 covers a crash after payment-link creation, AT-081 covers email failure, and AT-082 covers lost payout callbacks followed by reconciliation. A also tests recurring calendar boundaries and completion in AT-075 and AT-088, whereas B's AT-035–038 and AT-054 cover creation, scheduling, stopping, retries, and deduplication without those boundaries.
falsifiablesonnet-5-5-skill A's AT-058 specifies responses at 1.5 and 2.5 seconds under a two-second timeout, while B's AT-058 says to simulate a timeout without checking the cutoff from both sides. A's AT-059 explicitly contrasts 23-hour and over-24-hour stalled invoices, whereas B's AT-059 only states that invoices exceeding 24 hours can be detected; A therefore provides stronger checks against implementations that flag everything or time out too early.
levelssonnet-5-5-skill A separates server enforcement from browser interaction for missing tokens (AT-012/013), frozen clients (AT-014/015), and access control (AT-005/006). B places browser assertions such as 'no request sent,' disabled buttons, and toast messages in API integration tests under services/api/src/invoice/send.test.ts (AT-006/007), leaving those tests at a level that cannot directly observe the stated UI behavior.
traceablesonnet-5-5-none Both outputs provide covers anchors, but B additionally maps cases to concrete implementation test locations: AT-036 targets services/api/src/schedule/recurring.test.ts and AT-063 targets services/api/src/webhook/verify.test.ts. A's corresponding AT-057 and AT-063 provide requirement anchors but no implementation file or task mapping, so B offers a more explicit implementation handoff, although neither supplies task IDs.

Fields this run did not observe GAPS.md

  • ttft_ms: non-streaming calls; no time to first token.
  • cache: the provider reported cached prompt tokens on some calls; recorded under tokens.cached, no cache cost breakdown.
  • deployment_ref.region / tier: who served the model is read from the command's trace (cli/…), from OpenRouter's upstream provider, or from the model id a direct API answered with; region and service tier are not exposed.
  • human_intervention: not observed; no trace format read here says whether the agent waited on a person.
  • tool_calls, turns: observed for every trial from the trace the command left; counts in agent per row, the turn list in traces/.
  • checkpoint: not observed; no trace format read here says whether the agent resumed from one.
  • transport retry: model-API calls retry transient failures (HTTP 408/409/425/429, 5xx, network) twice with exponential backoff and no jitter; per-trial retry counts are not in scores.jsonl, only retry cost.
  • evaluator errors: none in this run.
  • absolute 1–5 scoring: **not run** — the spec declares methods without absolute-1-5. Magnitude is therefore not observed at all in this run; the empty score columns are a declaration, not a failure.
  • effective N: 0 of 3 inputs separated any two candidates on task success; 3 every candidate passed, 0 gave everyone the same partial rate. Only the separating inputs carry evidence for a choice (items.json). The judge preferred one candidate over another on 3 of 3.
  • pairwise judging uses only the first successful output per (candidate, input); repeated trials feed absolute scores only. The selector reads the pairwise score only to rank judge-preference, and to break a tie on assurance in highest-assurance.
  • statistical uncertainty: the chosen candidate's gated rates carry Wilson 95% intervals, and a highest-assurance pick must beat the runner-up on an exact sign test over inputs (recommendation.json evidence). Inputs generated from one template are still treated as independent, which overstates certainty when they share a template.
  • **combined run**: built from agb-skill-ablation-2026-11-17T07-37-55-127Z (later runs supersede earlier ones per trial); nothing was generated or judged for it. combined-from.json says which run each trial came from.
